Invoice extraction with accurate consistency guidelines

Correct packets: 3/3 baseline → 3/3 guided. Tool calls: [17, 17, 16] → [16, 39, 17]. Median: 17 → 17; range width: 1 → 23.

These reviewed accurate-consistency guidelines did not reduce variation in this three-pair set. All baseline and guided packets passed; neither arm had a final-output failure, so the earlier intermittent failure did not recur here.

Baseline calls were 17, 17 and 16 (50 total); guided calls were 16, 39 and 17 (72 total). Both medians were 17. The observed range width increased from 1 to 23 calls. These are descriptive results on one synthetic packet, without a reliable estimate of population reliability or a causal comparison with the earlier fast-guideline cohort.

The 39-call guided run first tried three nonexistent temporary paths, despite the path guideline. It then tried pypdf, PyPDF2, pdfplumber and pymupdf, none installed in the task environment; switched to pdftotext; investigated missing image-page text; and finally manually embedded the extracted data. It produced correct CSVs, but the extraction strategy and tool-selection detours remained variable.

All four reviewed guidelines were present in each guided retrieval, and Cortex recorded their serving IDs and original training source in the first request. The frozen selection and input PDF hashes remained unchanged. Native library source was not changed; the demo selects the existing accurate consistency processor explicitly.

Candidate 2, which recommends manual embedding rather than parsing libraries, was rejected before evaluation because it overgeneralizes from one successful training session. It might have discouraged the observed library detour in this constrained environment, but this experiment does not test that unpromoted candidate. Selection was not changed after seeing results.

A useful next experiment would learn from actual failed or detoured traces with explicit feedback about unavailable libraries and description preservation, then test fresh invoice packets. Replaying one passing training trajectory produced reusable bookkeeping advice but did not establish a reliable extraction procedure.

Task and Evolve models: aws/claude-sonnet-4-5 and aws/claude-sonnet-4-5. Claude Code 2.1.287, Cortex, Evolve, Poppler and Tesseract run inside Docker; inference uses the remote API.

Architecture

Task

Three invoices, five PDF pages, 16 items: text extraction, repeated table headings, a description continuing across a page boundary, an image-only second page, signed credits, a three-decimal price and a deliberately mismatched printed total. Every pair and repetition receives the same trial-1 PDF bytes and task text.

Extract every invoice line item from the PDFs in input/ into items.csv.
Use exactly these columns: invoice_id,vendor,description,quantity,unit_price,line_amount.
Preserve invoice IDs and complete item descriptions, including continuation text.
Include credits and discounts as signed line items. Do not include table headings,
page subtotals, tax, or grand totals as items. Preserve exact decimal amounts.

Reconcile each invoice's sum of line amounts plus its printed tax against its
printed total. Write reconciliation.csv with exactly these columns:
invoice_id,subtotal,tax,printed_total,computed_total,difference,status.
Difference means computed_total minus printed_total. Status must be matched
when the difference is zero and discrepancy otherwise. Report any discrepancy.

Keep all source PDFs unchanged. Use the installed local tools; do not install
packages or write persistent memory files. If using Python, run it with
uv run --offline --no-sync --project /opt/demo python.
Show the successful commands and briefly explain how you checked the result.

Learning and isolation

The previous guides used fast consistency generation. This experiment reuses its byte-identical, verified training trajectory with accurate generation: five samples on each of the first 15 replayable steps, default temperature 0.5, high uncertainty threshold 0.15, and the default skip-on-no-uncertainty gate. No evaluation recording or feedback is used for learning. Selection is frozen before six fresh batch sessions, ordered baseline/guided, guided/baseline, baseline/guided. Each has a fresh UUID, workspace and Claude configuration. Bash, Read, Write and Edit are permitted with no stdin or interactive permission prompts.

Cortex recordings lack PDF image bytes and hidden reasoning. Resampled prefixes therefore approximate the original decisions where visual input mattered. Resampling observes decision variation; fresh CSV grading establishes correctness. All tasks share a container/results volume, so filesystem permissions do not isolate sibling workspaces. The author-side oracle is absent from the task container.

Generated guidelines

Candidate 1 · promoted unchanged

When reading files from a relative path mentioned in a task description (e.g., 'PDFs in input/'), always construct the full path using the current working directory from the system message or by checking pwd first. Do not attempt to read from absolute paths that include temporary directory prefixes (like /tmp/...) unless explicitly confirmed.

Candidate 2 · not promoted

When extracting structured data from PDFs where precise decimal amounts, multi-line descriptions, and signed values (credits/discounts) must be preserved, embed the extracted data directly in a Python script rather than attempting to use intermediate parsing libraries. Manual extraction from PDF text content ensures complete control over continuation text, sign preservation, and decimal precision.

16. tool · toolu_bdrk_01MmspYVdZggLJ8ue4mpCrTE
import csv
import re
from decimal import Decimal
from pathlib import Path

# PDF text extraction using pypdf
from pypdf import PdfReader

def parse_amount(amount_str):
    """Parse amount string, handling parentheses for credits"""
    amount_str = amount_str.strip()
    is_negative = amount_str.startswith('(') and amount_str.endswith(')')
    if is_negative:
        amount_str = amount_str[1:-1]
    # Remove commas
    amount_str = amount_str.replace(',', '')
    value = Decimal(amount_str)
    return -value if is_negative else value

def extract_invoice_data(pdf_path):
    """Extract invoice line items and totals from a PDF"""
    reader = PdfReader(pdf_path)
    invoice_id = pdf_path.stem
    
    # Read all pages
    all_text = ""
    for page in reader.pages:
        all_text += page.extract_text() + "\n"
    
    # Extract vendor name (first line)
    lines = all_text.split('\n')
    vendor = lines[0].strip()
    
    # Extract subtotal, tax, and total
    subtotal_match = re.search(r'Subtotal\s+([\d,]+\.?\d*)', all_text)
    tax_match = re.search(r'Tax\s+([\d,]+\.?\d*)', all_text)
    total_match = re.search(r'Total due\s+([\d,]+\.?\d*)', all_text)
    
    subtotal = parse_amount(subtotal_match.group(1)) if subtotal_match else Decimal('0')
    tax = parse_amount(tax_match.group(1)) if tax_match else Decimal('0')
    total_due = parse_amount(total_match.group(1)) if total_match else Decimal('0')
    
    # Extract line items
    items = []
    
    # Pattern to match line items with quantity, unit price, and amount
    # The description may span multiple lines
    in_items_section = False
    current_item = None
    
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        line = line.strip()
        
        # Skip header lines
        if 'Description' in line and 'Qty' in line and 'Unit price' in line:
            in_items_section = True
            continue
        
        # Stop at subtotal line
        if line.startswith('Subtotal'):
            if current_item:
                items.append(current_item)
            break
        
        if not in_items_section:
            continue
        
        # Skip continuation notices
        if 'Invoice continues' in line or 'Totals are on the final page' in line:
            continue
        
        if 'Continuation of line items' in line:
            continue
        
        # Try to match a line item with qty, price, and amount
        # Pattern: description text followed by numbers
        match = re.match(r'^(.+?)\s+(\d+\.?\d*)\s+([\d,]+\.?\d*|\(\d+\.?\d*\))\s+([\d,]+\.?\d*|\([\d,]+\.?\d*\))$', line)
        
        if match:
            # Save previous item if exists
            if current_item:
                items.append(current_item)
            
            description = match.group(1).strip()
            qty = Decimal(match.group(2))
            unit_price = parse_amount(match.group(3))
            line_amount = parse_amount(match.group(4))
            
            current_item = {
                'invoice_id': invoice_id,
                'vendor': vendor,
                'description': description,
                'quantity': qty,
                'unit_price': unit_price,
                'line_amount': line_amount
            }
        elif current_item and line and not line.startswith('INVOICE') and not line.startswith('Bill to') and not line.startswith('Currency') and not line.startswith('Page'):
            # This might be a continuation of the description
            # Check if this line has no numbers or is just text
            if not re.search(r'^\d+\.?\d*\s+[\d,]+', line):
                current_item['description'] += ' ' + line
    
    return {
        'vendor': vendor,
        'items': items,
        'subtotal': subtotal,
        'tax': tax,
        'total_due': total_due
    }

def main():
    input_dir = Path('input')
    pdf_files = sorted(input_dir.glob('*.pdf'))
    
    all_items = []
    reconciliation = []
    
    for pdf_path in pdf_files:
        print(f"Processing {pdf_path.name}...")
        data = extract_invoice_data(pdf_path)
        
        # Add items
        all_items.extend(data['items'])
        
        # Reconciliation
        computed_subtotal = sum(item['line_amount'] for item in data['items'])
        computed_total = computed_subtotal + data['tax']
        difference = computed_total - data['total_due']
        status = 'matched' if difference == 0 else 'discrepancy'
        
        reconciliation.append({
            'invoice_id': pdf_path.stem,
            'subtotal': computed_subtotal,
            'tax': data['tax'],
            'printed_total': data['total_due'],
            'computed_total': computed_total,
            'difference': difference,
            'status': status
        })
        
        if status == 'discrepancy':
            print(f"  WARNING: Discrepancy found in {pdf_path.stem}!")
            print(f"    Computed total: {computed_total}")
            print(f"    Printed total: {data['total_due']}")
            print(f"    Difference: {difference}")
    
    # Write items.csv
    with open('items.csv', 'w', newline='') as f:
        writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=['invoice_id', 'vendor', 'description', 'quantity', 'unit_price', 'line_amount'])
        writer.writeheader()
        for item in all_items:
            writer.writerow(item)
    
    print(f"\nWrote {len(all_items)} items to items.csv")
    
    # Write reconciliation.csv
    with open('reconciliation.csv', 'w', newline='') as f:
        writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=['invoice_id', 'subtotal', 'tax', 'printed_total', 'computed_total', 'difference', 'status'])
        writer.writeheader()
        for rec in reconciliation:
            writer.writerow(rec)
    
    print(f"Wrote {len(reconciliation)} reconciliation records to reconciliation.csv")
    
    # Report discrepancies
    discrepancies = [r for r in reconciliation if r['status'] == 'discrepancy']
    if discrepancies:
        print(f"\n{len(discrepancies)} DISCREPANCY(IES) FOUND:")
        for d in discrepancies:
            print(f"  Invoice {d['invoice_id']}: difference = {d['difference']}")
    else:
        print("\nAll invoices reconciled successfully!")

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

## Verified Extraction and Checking Procedure Summary

### Successful Approach

**1. PDF Reading and Data Extraction:**
- Used the `Read` tool to examine all 3 PDFs (001071.pdf, 001082.pdf, 001093.pdf)
- Manually extracted invoice data from the PDF text content, carefully preserving:
  - Complete multi-line descriptions with continuation text (invoice 001082's seal kit description spanned pages)
  - Signed negative values for credits/discounts (-9.80, -35.00, -25.00)
  - Exact decimal precision (2.5, 4.375, 1245.60)
  - Vendor names from invoice headers

**2. CSV Generation:**
- Created Python script that directly encoded the extracted data
- Used `uv run --offline --no-sync --project /opt/demo python` as required (no package installation)
- Generated **items.csv** with exact column specification: invoice_id, vendor, description, quantity, unit_price, line_amount
- Excluded all subtotals, tax rows, and grand totals as instructed

**3. Reconciliation:**
- Used Python's `Decimal` type for precise financial calculations
- Computed totals as: subtotal (sum of line amounts) + tax
- Compared against printed totals
- Generated **reconciliation.csv** with difference = computed_total - printed_total
- Status logic: "matched" when difference = 0, "discrepancy" otherwise

**4. Verification:**
- Confirmed 16 line items extracted (4 from 001071, 6 from 001082, 6 from 001093)
- Detected and reported 1 discrepancy: Invoice 001093 off by -$0.01
- Verified source PDFs unchanged via checksums

### Approaches That Failed

**1. File Path Error:**
- Initial attempt to read PDFs failed because I used relative `input/` path instead of full `/results/tasks/training/input/` path
- Fixed by checking `pwd` and using absolute paths

**2. Display Formatting:**
- Attempted to use `column` command for table formatting, but it wasn't installed
- Not a critical failure—used `sed` alternative for display purposes only

### Key Success Factors

- Manual data extraction from PDF text avoided OCR/parsing complexity
- Decimal arithmetic prevented floating-point rounding errors in reconciliation
- Exact adherence to column names and CSV structure
- Proper handling of negative values for credits/discounts
- Complete description preservation including continuation text across pages

All 16 items and 3 reconciliation rows confirmed to match reference data.

Limits

Three pairs on one synthetic packet are exploratory. Call-count stability is separate from output correctness; no causal or population-level claim. Cortex first-request attribution establishes recorded injection; the final outbound payload is not independently inspected. Counted calls are recorded main-agent tool calls, including errors and retries; hidden reasoning is unavailable. The result does not separate advice content from the extra context it adds or compare fast and accurate guides on simultaneous identical cohorts.
